<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove the quadratic strong-operator paving conjecture of Popa and Vaes. For every $`0\lt \varepsilon \lt 1`$, every self-adjoint element of a von Neumann algebra admits strong-operator paving over each maximal abelian subalgebra with at most $`5\times10^8\varepsilon ^{-2}`$ projections. The bound is uniform over representations and requires no separability or conditional-expectation assumption.

- **claim-main-paving** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): For every $0<\varepsilon<1$, set $$r_\varepsilon=\left\lceil400000000\varepsilon^{-2}\right\rceil +\left\lceil4/\varepsilon\right\rceil+1 \le500000000\varepsilon^{-2}.$$ For every masa $A\subseteq M\subseteq\mathcal B(H)$ and every $x=x^*\in M$, the element $x$ is $(\varepsilon,r_\varepsilon)$ so-pavable over $A$. In particular, the number of projections is fixed before the inclusion, operator, and strong neighborhood. No separability or conditional-expectation assumption is imposed on the inclusion.
  - Explanation: Resolves the quadratic strong-operator paving conjecture. One explicit number of projections, depending only on epsilon, works for every masa in every represented von Neumann algebra and every self-adjoint element, with no separability or expectation assumption.
- **claim-ultrapower-norm-paving** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-ultrapower-paving)): Let $A\subseteq M$ be a masa. Suppose that $A$ is countably decomposable and is the range of a normal conditional expectation from $M$. Let $\omega$ be a free ultrafilter on $\mathbb N$, and let $A^\omega\subseteq M^\omega$ be the associated inclusion in the Ocneanu von Neumann ultrapower. For every $0<\varepsilon<1$ and every $x=x^*\in M^\omega$, there are a partition $p_1,\ldots,p_{r_\varepsilon}\in A^\omega$ and an element $a=a^*\in A^\omega$ such that $$\\|a\\|\le\\|x\\|, \qquad \left\\|\sum_{i=1}^{r_\varepsilon}p_i x p_i-a\right\\|\le\varepsilon\\|x\\|.$$ Here $r_\varepsilon$ is the same integer as in Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>.
  - Explanation: For countably decomposable masas that are ranges of normal expectations, the same projection count gives genuine norm paving inside the Ocneanu ultrapower.
- **claim-no-central-corner** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-no-central)): Let $A\subseteq M$ be a masa such that $M$ has no nonzero $A$-central normal positive functional. Let $x=x^*\in M$ satisfy $\\|x\\|\leq1$, and let $0<\varepsilon<1$. Put $r_{\rm p}=\lceil4/\varepsilon\rceil$. For every normal state $\varphi$ on $M$ and every $\rho>0$ there are a partition $p_1,\ldots,p_{r_{\rm p}}$ in $A$, an $a=a^*\in A$ with $\\|a\\|\leq1$, and a projection $q\in M$ such that $$\varphi(1-q)<\rho, \qquad \left\\|q\left(\sum_{i=1}^{r_{\rm p}}p_i x p_i-a\right)q\right\\| \leq\varepsilon.$$
  - Explanation: When no nonzero central normal positive functional exists, a partition with a count of order inverse epsilon and a compression of arbitrarily large state mass paves the operator, via a symmetric Hilbert-space estimate and two randomizations.
- **claim-state-reduction** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-state-reduction)): Fix $0<\varepsilon<1$ and an integer $R\geq1$. Suppose that for every masa $A\subseteq M$ admitting a faithful $A$-central normal state $\varphi$, every self-adjoint contraction $x\in M$, and every $\rho>0$, there exist a partition of $1$ into $R$ projections of $A$, a self-adjoint $a\in A$ of norm at most one, and a projection $q\in M$ with $\varphi(1-q)<\rho$ and $$\left\\|q\left(\sum_{i=1}^R p_i x p_i-a\right)q\right\\|\leq\varepsilon.$$ Then every masa in every represented von Neumann algebra has the strong-operator paving property of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> at error $\varepsilon$, with $$r=R+\lceil4/\varepsilon\rceil+1.$$ In particular this number is chosen before the finite vector set and the strong-neighborhood tolerance.
  - Explanation: Reduces the general case to inclusions carrying a faithful central normal state: corner partitions with disjoint palettes are combined and the counts add, independently of the strong neighborhood.
- **claim-pinching-limit** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-pinching)): Let $A\subseteq M$ be a masa and $\varphi$ a faithful $A$-central normal state. There is a faithful normal $\varphi$-preserving conditional expectation $E_A\colon M\to A$. On bounded subsets of $M$, convergence to zero in $\\|\cdot\\|_\varphi$ is equivalent to strong convergence in the faithful GNS representation. For every $z\in M$, $$\label{eq:pinching-limit} \\|S_P(z)-E_A(z)\\|_\varphi\longrightarrow0$$ as the finite partitions $P$ of $A$ refine. The same conclusion holds along any increasing sequence of finite partitions generating $A$.
  - Explanation: With a faithful central state, pinchings along refining partitions converge in the state norm to the state-preserving conditional expectation onto the masa, equivalently strongly in the GNS representation.
- **claim-separable-reduction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-separable-reduction)): Let $A\subseteq M$ be a masa with a faithful $A$-central normal state $\varphi$, and let $x=x^*\in M$. There exist unital von Neumann subalgebras $A_0\subseteq A$ and $M_0\subseteq M$ such that $x\in M_0$, $M_0$ has separable predual, $A_0$ is a masa in $M_0$, and $E_A(M_0)\subseteq A_0$. The restriction of $\varphi$ is faithful, normal, and $A_0$-central. Thus a uniform faithful-state paving result for inclusions with separable predual implies the same result without that restriction.
  - Explanation: Shows that the faithful-state problem can be moved to countably generated subalgebras with separable predual in which the subalgebra stays maximal abelian.
- **claim-type-i-normalizers** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-type-i-normalizers)): Let $D$ be a type I von Neumann algebra with separable predual, and let $C\subseteq D$ be a masa. Suppose $D$ admits a faithful $C$-central normal state. Then the groupoid normalizers of $C$ generate $D$. Every abelian projection of $D$ is Murray–von Neumann equivalent in $D$ to a projection of $C$.
  - Explanation: In a type I algebra with a faithful central normal state, groupoid normalizers of the masa generate the algebra and abelian projections are equivalent to projections of the masa.
- **claim-atomless-kernels** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-atomless-kernels)): In the setting of this section, for every $y\in M^\circ$ the map $$K_y(f)=E_A(y^*fy),\qquad f\in A,$$ has a positive kernel whose mass is at most $\\|y\\|^2$ and which is atomless for almost every output point $v\in X$. For $k\geq1$ and $y_1,\ldots,y_k\in M^\circ$, the composition $K_{y_1}\circ\cdots\circ K_{y_k}$ has a positive kernel whose mass is at most $\prod_{j=1}^k\\|y_j\\|^2$ and which is atomless almost everywhere. If $p_{\rm at}\in A$ is the projection of the atomic part of $A$, then $$p_{\rm at}y=yp_{\rm at}=0\qquad(y\in M^\circ).$$
  - Explanation: Compressions of elements outside the normalizing algebra to the masa are given by positive kernels that are atomless almost everywhere, also after composition; such elements annihilate the atomic part. This removes coincidences in the later moment transfer.
- **claim-mixed-bounds** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-mixed-bounds)): For a $k$-tuple of Hermitian matrices $A_1,\ldots,A_k$ on a common nonempty finite set, let $$\chi[A_1,\ldots,A_k](z) =\mathbb E\prod_{j=1}^k\det(zI-A_j\|_{S_j}),$$ where each site is independently assigned uniformly to one of the $k$ sets $S_j$, and the determinant of an empty matrix is $1$. This polynomial is real-rooted. If $k\geq2$ and the $A_j$ are zero-diagonal contractions, then $$\lambda_{\max}\chi[A_1,\ldots,A_k] <\frac{3\sqrt2}{\sqrt{k}}. \label{eq:poly-mixed-upper}$$ For any zero-diagonal Hermitian matrix $L$, without a norm restriction, $$\lambda_{\max}\chi[L,-L]\geq\frac{\\|L\\|}{2}. \label{eq:poly-mixed-lower}$$ Consequently, on a nonempty finite set, if every site is unassigned, the unit-weight polynomial without diagonal shifts has largest root $<3/\sqrt r$. If every site is assigned, that unit-weight unshifted polynomial is $$\prod_{i=1}^r\chi[T\|_{c^{-1}(i)},-T\|_{c^{-1}(i)}],$$ where factors on empty color classes are $1$.
  - Explanation: Records the finite-dimensional mixed determinantal input: real-rootedness, an upper bound on the largest root for zero-diagonal contractions, and a lower bound tied to the norm for a matrix paired with its negative.
- **claim-polynomial-quotients** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-polynomial-quotients)): For the finite data above, $$P_I(Z)= \left(\prod_{v\in I}\frac{1}{k_v!} \partial_{z_v}^{\,k_v-1}\right) \prod_j\det\bigl((Z-B_j)\|_{U_j}\bigr), \qquad \partial_{z_v}P_I=P_{-v}. \label{eq:poly-differential}$$ More generally, use any matrices $B_j$ on $U_j$ in the same independent-choice construction. If $\operatorname{Im}B_j\leq hI$ for a real number $h$, then $P_I(Z)$ and every deletion polynomial are nonzero whenever $\operatorname{Im}z_v>h$ at every remaining site. In this region, $$\operatorname{Im}\frac{P_I(Z)}{P_{-v}(Z\|_{I\setminus\{v\}})} \geq\operatorname{Im}z_v-h. \label{eq:poly-half-plane}$$* *Return to the Hermitian data in Equation <a href="#eq:poly-definition" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:poly-definition">[eq:poly-definition]</a>. For $v\in I$, set $$Q_v=P_{-v},\qquad F_v(z)=\frac{P_I(z)}{Q_v(z)}, \qquad G_v(z)=\frac{Q_v(z)}{P_I(z)}.$$ These rational functions are analytic in the upper half-plane, and $$F_v(z)=z+a_v-\sum_{\lambda}\frac{b_{v,\lambda}}{z-\lambda}, \qquad b_{v,\lambda}\geq0, \label{eq:poly-arrow-quotient}$$ where $\lambda$ ranges over the distinct roots of $Q_v$. Let $\Lambda_v$ be the diagonal matrix listing those roots with multiplicity. There is a vector $g_v$, with squared coordinate $b_{v,\lambda}$ on one copy of each $\lambda$ and zero on its other copies, such that $$H_v=\begin{pmatrix}-a_v&g_v^*\\ g_v&\Lambda_v\end{pmatrix} \label{eq:poly-arrow}$$ has characteristic polynomial $P_I$. Its first-coordinate spectral measure $\rho_v$ is a probability measure satisfying $$G_v(z)=\int_{\mathbb R}\frac{1}{z-x}\,\rho_v(dx),\qquad \sum_{v\in I}\rho_v=\sum_{P_I(x)=0}\delta_x. \label{eq:poly-root-measures}$$ The roots on the right are counted with multiplicity, and $Q_v$ interlaces $P_I$.
  - Explanation: Derivative identities turn the averaged polynomial into a deletion-compatible object; half-plane nonvanishing gives real roots, an arrow-matrix representation, rooted spectral measures that sum to the root-counting measure, and interlacing.
- **claim-finite-cost-endpoints** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-finite-cost-endpoints)): For the finite data of this section, the following hold.* 1. *If every site is unassigned and $t>3/\sqrt r$, then $P_I(x)>0$ for every $x\geq0$, and $s(P_I)=0$. This includes $I=\varnothing$, with $P_\varnothing=1$.* 2. *Suppose $I\ne\varnothing$ and every site is assigned, and put $$T_c=\sum_{i=1}^r 1_{\{c=i\}}T1_{\{c=i\}}.$$ For every $\alpha>0$, if $\\|T_c\\|>2\beta t+\alpha$, then $$\lambda_{\max}P_I\geq\frac\alpha2\min_{v\in I}W_v, \qquad s(P_I)\geq\frac\alpha2\min_{v\in I}W_v.$$
  - Explanation: With nothing assigned and a large enough shift the positive-root cost is zero; on a fully assigned block, excessive pinched norm forces a positive largest root and positive cost.
- **claim-deletion-cost-tails** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-positive-cost)): For the finite Hermitian data above, let $n_P(x)=\#\{\lambda:P(\lambda)=0,\ \lambda>x\}$, with multiplicity. Then, for every $v\in I$, $$\begin{aligned} 0&\leq n_{P_I}(x)-n_{Q_v}(x)\leq1, \label{eq:poly-interlacing-count}\\ d_v&=\int_0^\infty(n_{P_I}-n_{Q_v})(x)\,dx, \label{eq:poly-cost-integral}\\ \int_B^\infty(n_{P_I}-n_{Q_v})(x)\,dx &\leq 2\int(x-B)_+\,\rho_v(dx) +(a_v+B)\rho_v((B,\infty)),\qquad B\geq0. \label{eq:poly-cost-tail} \end{aligned}$$ In particular $0\leq d_v\leq2m_v+a_v$. All these conclusions hold for either modification at $v$, with its own shift and rooted measure.
  - Explanation: Expresses the deletion increment as an integral of a root-count difference, controls its tail by the rooted measure and shift, and bounds the increment by a multiple of the rooted cost plus the shift.
- **claim-rooted-locality** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-rooted-locality)): Let a graph support the finite zero-diagonal Hermitian contraction $T$. The coefficient of $z^{-1-k}$ in $G_v(z)$ is $\int x^k\,\rho_v(dx)$ and is determined by the data on the radius-$k$ ball about $v$. The Laurent coefficients of $F_v(z)$, and of the two options $F^0_{v,i}(z)$ and $F_{v,i}(z)$ at an unassigned site, likewise depend only on a radius bounded by their order. Moreover, $$\int x^2\,\rho_v(dx) =a_v^2+\sum_{u\ne v}W_vW_u\|T_{vu}\|^2 \mathbb P(\omega_u=\omega_v), \qquad \sum_\lambda b_{v,\lambda} =\sum_{u\ne v}W_vW_u\|T_{vu}\|^2 \mathbb P(\omega_u=\omega_v). \label{eq:poly-second-moment}$$ The same identities hold for either site option, using the corresponding law of the pairs and diagonal shift.* *If neighboring weights have ratios in $[K^{-1},K]$, $K\geq1$, then for all these data $$\int x^2\,\rho_v(dx)\leq\bigl((\beta t)^2+K\bigr)W_v^2,\quad m_v\leq C_0W_v,\quad d_v\leq(2C_0+\beta t)W_v, \quad C_0=\sqrt{(\beta t)^2+K}. \label{eq:poly-weight-bounds}$$ If all weights are at most $H_0$, every polynomial and every principal restriction has all its roots in $$,\qquad L=(1+\beta t)H_0. \label{eq:poly-root-support}$$ The rooted measures have the same support bound.
  - Explanation: Rooted moments depend only on bounded graph balls, the second moment has an explicit collision formula, and neighboring-ratio assumptions give weight-proportional bounds on costs and a uniform root support.
- **claim-single-site-assignment** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-assignment)): For a finite zero-diagonal Hermitian contraction $T$, arbitrary positive weights, $r\geq1$, $t>0$, and a partial coloring as above, let $v$ be unassigned. With $\beta=80$, there is $i\in\{1,\ldots,r\}$ such that $$d_v^{\,i}-d_v\leq24m_v. \label{eq:poly-assignment}$$ The bound is independent of the finite set and its weights.
  - Explanation: At an unassigned site some color can be assigned permanently while increasing the deletion cost by at most a fixed multiple of the rooted cost, independently of the finite set and weights.
- **claim-bounded-limits** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-bounded-costs)): For the invariant measured graph data on the finite measure space $(X,\nu)$ specified above, the rooted measures, transforms, and costs have limits as finite principal subsets exhaust the graph component of the root. Denote them by $\rho_v$, $G_v$, $F_v$, $m_v$, and $d_v$. They are independent of the exhaustion and are measurable in $v$. At each present vertex, $\rho_v$ is a probability measure supported on $[-L,L]$, and, for $z$ in the open upper half-plane, $$G_v(z)=\int\frac{1}{z-x}\,\rho_v(dx),\qquad F_v(z)=\frac1{G_v(z)}.$$ The same assertions hold after conditioning the color at one unassigned vertex, with either its original shift or its increased shift.* *For fixed $D_0,H_0,r,t$, the costs $m_v,d_v$ and the virtual shifted costs $d_v^{\,i}$, $1\leq i\leq r$, are uniformly approximable by calculations on a ball of finite radius about $v$. The approximation radius is independent of the particular graph, weights, partial coloring, and set of deleted vertices. These statements apply to a finite-measure restriction of an invariant measured relation as well.
  - Explanation: On bounded weighted invariant measured graphs, rooted measures, transforms and costs exist as exhaustion-independent limits, are measurable, and are uniformly approximable by calculations on balls of fixed radius.
- **claim-integral-change** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-integral-change)): Under the bounded finite-measure hypotheses of Lemma <a href="#lem:bounded-costs" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:bounded-costs">15</a>, let the old and new data differ only by changes of partial coloring or deletion at a measurable set $E_0$ of vertices. Fix a measurable real-valued function that is injective on $E_0$ in each relation class, and order these vertices by its values. At $u\in E_0$, use new data at all earlier vertices and old data at all later vertices. Let $\delta d_u$ be the new-minus-old difference of the single-vertex deletion cost at $u$ in this environment, with cost zero when $u$ is absent. Then $$\label{inv:integral-change} \int_X(m_v^{\mathrm{new}}-m_v^{\mathrm{old}})\,d\nu(v) =\int_{E_0}\delta d_u\,d\nu(u).$$ In particular, deletion alone cannot increase the integral of $m_v$.
  - Explanation: The integral change of rooted cost under ordered updates equals the integral of single-vertex deletion cost differences in comparison environments; deletion alone cannot raise the integral.
- **claim-band-comparison** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-rank-comparison)): Consider the invariant measured graph data on the finite measure space $(X,\nu)$ specified above and assume, in addition, that neighboring weights have ratios in $[K^{-1},K]$, where $K\geq1$. Fix $0<b_1<b_2<\infty$ and the band $$\mathcal U=\{v:b_1\leq W_v\leq b_2\}.$$ Delete a measurable set $E_{\mathrm{hi}}$ disjoint from $\mathcal U$, without changing the coloring at retained vertices. Let $\delta_{\mathrm{tot}}$ denote the difference between the original and the deleted configurations, in either orientation. For each compact set $\Omega$ in the open upper half-plane there is a constant $C$ such that, for every $z\in\Omega$ and $1\leq i\leq r$, $$\label{inv:rank-comparison} \int_{\mathcal U}\|\delta_{\mathrm{tot}}G_v(z)\|\,d\nu(v) +\int_{\mathcal U\cap\{c=0\}} \|\delta_{\mathrm{tot}}J_i(v,z)\|\,d\nu(v) \leq C\nu(E_{\mathrm{hi}}).$$ Only band vertices present before deletion are included in these integrals. The constant may depend on $\Omega,r,t,\beta,D_0,K,b_1,b_2$, but not on $H_0$, the measure or its total mass, the partial coloring, the graph itself, or the deleted set.
  - Explanation: Deleting vertices outside a fixed weight band changes the transforms inside the band by an amount bounded by the measure of the deleted set, with a constant independent of the largest weight.
- **claim-bernoulli-coloring** (construction, [source](#stmt-thm-bernoulli-coloring)): Let $(X,\mu,\mathcal R)$, $G$, $\Delta$, and $T$ satisfy the assumptions above: $\mathcal R$ is countable and nonsingular on a standard probability space, $G$ is undirected, has no loops, has degree at most $D_0<\infty$, and satisfies Equation <a href="#eq:coloring-edge-ratios" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:coloring-edge-ratios">[eq:coloring-edge-ratios]</a>, and $T$ is a measurable Hermitian contraction with zero diagonal and support on $G$, allowing diagonal unitary conjugacy under a change of root. Fix an integer $r\geq1$ and a real number $t>3/\sqrt r$, and put $\beta=80$.* *For every integer $m\geq1$ and every $\alpha,\eta>0$, there is a measurable coloring $c:\widetilde X\to\{1,\ldots,r\}$ on the relative Bernoulli extension such that $$\kappa_v\{\omega:c(v,\omega)=i\}=\frac1r \quad\text{for every }i\in\{1,\ldots,r\} \quad\text{and almost every }v\in X, \label{eq:coloring-marginal}$$ and $$\widetilde\mu\left\{(v,\omega): \\|T_{B_G(v,m),c}\\|\leq2\beta t+\alpha\right\}>1-\eta. \label{eq:coloring-ball-conclusion}$$
  - Explanation: On the relative Bernoulli extension there is a measurable coloring with uniform conditional color marginals making the pinched matrix small on any prescribed finite-radius ball with high probability.
- **claim-homogeneous-costs** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-homogeneous-costs)): Fix $D_0,K,r,t$ and $\beta=80$, and let the probability space, relation, graph, matrix data, and partial coloring satisfy the assumptions above. For each field $f$ in the finite list $m,d,d^1,\ldots,d^r$, there is a bounded measurable function $\bar f$ on $X$ such that, on every fixed band $$\mathcal U_{b_1,b_2}=\{(v,s):b_1\leq W(v,s)\leq b_2\}, \qquad 0<b_1<b_2<\infty,$$ one has $$\frac{f^{l,H}}{W}\longrightarrow\bar f(v) \quad\text{in }L^1(\mathcal U_{b_1,b_2},\nu) \quad\text{jointly as }l\downarrow0, H\uparrow\infty. \label{eq:coloring-band-limit}$$ The errors tend to zero uniformly over all such probability bases and configurations with the fixed parameters and bounds. There is a constant $C_0$, depending only on those parameters, such that all the normalized cutoff fields on retained sites and all their limits have absolute value at most $C_0$. The limit fields are independent of height and satisfy $$\min_{1\leq i\leq r}\bar d^{i}-\bar d \leq24\bar m \quad\text{almost everywhere on }\{c=0\}. \label{eq:coloring-limit-assignment}$$
  - Explanation: After removing weight cutoffs on the invariant lift, normalized costs converge on every band to height-independent bounded limits that satisfy the single-site assignment inequality.
- **claim-strip-locality** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-strip-locality)): Under the assumptions of Lemma <a href="#lem:homogeneous-costs" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:homogeneous-costs">19</a>, put $$\Sigma_n=\{e^{-n}\leq W\leq e^n\}, \qquad f_{\Sigma_n}=f^{e^{-n},e^n}.$$ For every field $f$ in the list $m,d,d^1,\ldots,d^r$, $$\frac1{2n}\int_{\Sigma_n} \|f_{\Sigma_n}-W\bar f\|\,d\nu\longrightarrow0 \quad(n\to\infty), \label{eq:coloring-strip}$$ uniformly over the configurations and probability bases with the fixed parameters.* *If $c_1,c_2$ are two partial colorings on the same base with the same graph, matrix, and cocycle data, and $E_R$ is the set where they agree on the radius-$R$ graph ball, then $$\int_{E_R}\|\bar f(c_1)-\bar f(c_2)\|\,d\mu \leq\gamma(R), \qquad \gamma(R)\longrightarrow0, \label{eq:coloring-locality}$$ where the same function $\gamma$ may be used for the finite list of fields and is uniform over these configurations.* *Both assertions hold after taking a product with an auxiliary probability space and keeping the auxiliary parameter fixed along the equivalence relation. The cost fields may be chosen measurably on such product spaces.
  - Explanation: Strip averages of cost fields converge to the homogeneous limits, and two colorings agreeing on a large ball have close limit costs there.
- **claim-bad-balls** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-bad-balls)): Assume the hypotheses and parameter choices of Theorem <a href="#thm:bernoulli-coloring" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:bernoulli-coloring">18</a>. The all-unassigned partial coloring has $\mathcal S=0$. Fix $m\geq1$ and $\alpha>0$. For any measurable partial coloring $c$, let $\operatorname{Bad}_m(c)$ be the set of vertices $v$ for which every vertex of $B_G(v,m)$ is assigned and $$\\|T_{B_G(v,m),c}\\|>2\beta t+\alpha.$$ There is a finite constant $C_4$, depending only on $m,D_0,K,\alpha$, such that $$\mu(\operatorname{Bad}_m(c))\leq C_4\mathcal S(c). \label{eq:coloring-bad-balls}$$ The same assertions hold on probability extensions with the pulled-back graph, matrix, and cocycle data.
  - Explanation: The all-unassigned coloring has zero total cost, and the measure of vertices whose fully assigned ball violates the norm bound is controlled by the total cost.
- **claim-hazard-step** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-hazard-step)): Under the hypotheses of Theorem <a href="#thm:bernoulli-coloring" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:bernoulli-coloring">18</a>, let $c$ be any partial coloring determined before the fresh times, and choose $i(v)$ and $c^\theta$ as just described. There is a nonnegative function $g$, depending only on the fixed graph and polynomial parameters, such that $g(h)\to0$ as $h\downarrow0$ and, for every $0<h<1/2$, $$\mathcal S(c^h) \leq(1+24h)\mathcal S(c)+h g(h). \label{eq:coloring-step-bound}$$ The same function $g$ works for all such steps and current configurations.
  - Explanation: One random assignment step of small intensity raises the total cost by a controlled factor plus a vanishing error, uniformly over current configurations.
- **claim-bernoulli-model** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-bernoulli-model)): The pullback of base kernel operators identifies $N$ with a von Neumann subalgebra of $\widetilde N$. There is a faithful normal conditional expectation $$\widetilde E_N:\widetilde N\longrightarrow N$$ whose restriction to $B$ integrates labels conditional on the base point. If $\widetilde\varphi$ denotes integration of the diagonal in $\widetilde N$, then $$(\varphi\|_N)\circ\widetilde E_N=\widetilde\varphi.$$ Moreover, the reduced amalgamated free product $$\mathcal K=M*_N\widetilde N$$ has a faithful normal conditional expectation $E_N^{\mathcal K}$ onto $N$, restricting to $E_N$ and $\widetilde E_N$. The state $$\Phi=(\varphi\|_N)\circ E_N^{\mathcal K}$$ is faithful and normal, restricts to $\varphi$ and $\widetilde\varphi$ on the two constituent algebras, and satisfies $B\subset\mathcal K_\Phi$. The two constituent algebras are free over $N$: the expectation of every alternating product of letters from $\ker E_N$ and $\ker\widetilde E_N$ is zero.
  - Explanation: Embeds the normalizing algebra in the extended relation algebra, builds the reduced amalgamated free product over it with a faithful normal state, and shows the two constituents are free over the normalizing algebra.
- **claim-free-compression** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-free-compression)): In the model of Lemma <a href="#lem:bernoulli-model" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:bernoulli-model">23</a>, let $p\in B$ be a projection such that $\widetilde E_N(p)=\lambda1$, where $0\le\lambda\le1$. For every $w\in M^\circ$, $$\\|pwp\\|\le(\lambda+2\sqrt\lambda)\\|w\\|. \label{eq:free-compression}$$
  - Explanation: A color projection with constant conditional expectation compresses every element of the kernel of the normalizer expectation to a norm controlled by a function of that constant.
- **claim-state-multipliers** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-state-multipliers)): Let $\varphi$ be a faithful normal state on a von Neumann algebra $M$. For every $C<\infty$, every fixed $z\in M$, and every $\eta>0$, there is $\delta>0$ such that $$\label{eq:uniform-fixed-right} \\|D\\|\leq C,\quad\\|D\\|_\varphi<\delta \quad\Longrightarrow\quad \\|Dz\\|_\varphi<\eta.$$ The choice of $\delta$ is uniform over all such $D$. For $U,D\in M$ and $b\in M_\varphi$, $$\label{eq:state-left-centralizer} \\|UD\\|_\varphi\leq\\|U\\|\\|D\\|_\varphi, \qquad \\|Db\\|_\varphi\leq\\|b\\|\\|D\\|_\varphi.$$ More generally, the right multiplier bound is uniform when $z$ ranges over a norm-bounded set that is precompact in state norm. Consequently, a bounded family of errors tending uniformly to zero in state norm remains so after right multiplication by a fixed finite word whose factors range over such precompact sets or over bounded subsets of the centralizer. In particular, any specified bounded sequence converging to a fixed operator in state norm can be used as a right factor uniformly over its entire sequence. These conclusions permit bounded strong-\* approximation of fixed letters in fixed-length scalar words, uniformly in the intervening bounded centralizer factors and in the independently varying indices of other specified approximation sequences.
  - Explanation: Right multiplication by a fixed operator preserves smallness in the state norm uniformly over bounded errors; left multiplication by centralizer elements is controlled by the norm; this extends to fixed-length words.
- **claim-moment-transfer** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-moment-transfer)): Let $A\subset M$, $\varphi$, and $\mathcal K$ be as in Lemma <a href="#lem:bernoulli-model" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:bernoulli-model">23</a>. Fix a finite symbol size $s$, the corresponding cylinder algebra $\mathcal Y$, and the generating partition sequence $(\mathcal P_j)$ just described. For any fixed integer $k\ge1$ and fixed factors $Z_1,\ldots,Z_k$, each chosen in $M$ or in $\mathcal Y$, define $Z_h^{(j)}=Z_h$ for a factor chosen in $M$ and $Z_h^{(j)}=\mathrm{ev}_{\mathcal P_j}(Z_h)$ for a factor chosen in $\mathcal Y$. Use the same independent symbols for all evaluations in a word. Then $$\lim_{j\to\infty} \mathbb E_{\{z_a\}} \varphi\bigl(Z_1^{(j)}\cdots Z_k^{(j)}\bigr) =\Phi(Z_1\cdots Z_k). \label{eq:moment-transfer}$$
  - Explanation: Fixed scalar moments of words mixing letters from the algebra and finite cylinder functions are limits of averaged moments obtained by evaluating the cylinder functions on partitions of the masa.
- **claim-cylinder-partitions** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-cylinder-partitions)): In the Bernoulli extension of Lemma <a href="#lem:bernoulli-model" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:bernoulli-model">23</a>, let $p_1,\ldots,p_r\in B$ be projections with $\sum_i p_i=1$ and $p_ip_h=0$ for $i\ne h$. For every $\delta>0$ there is a projection partition $b_1,\ldots,b_r\in B$, each of whose members is a finite cylinder function for one common finite dyadic symbol size, such that $$\\|b_i-p_i\\|_\Phi<\delta\qquad(1\le i\le r).$$ Every evaluation of this cylinder partition by the preceding finite-symbol substitution gives a projection partition of $1$ in $A$, modulo the fixed null sets of its definition.
  - Explanation: Any projection partition in the Bernoulli extension is approximated in state norm by a finite cylinder partition of one dyadic symbol size, whose evaluations are genuine projection partitions in the masa.
- **claim-multiplier-calculus** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-multiplier-calculus)): Let $\mathcal L$ be a von Neumann algebra with a faithful normal state $\omega$. Let $\mathscr R_\omega$ consist of all uniformly bounded sequences $(R_n)$ in $\mathcal L$ such that $$\\|D_nR_n\\|_\omega\longrightarrow0$$ whenever $(D_n)$ is uniformly bounded and $\\|D_n\\|_\omega\to0$. Then $\mathscr R_\omega$ is an algebra, closed for the supremum operator norm. It contains all constant sequences and all uniformly bounded sequences in the centralizer of $\omega$. It is also closed under adding uniformly bounded sequences converging to zero in $\\|\cdot\\|_\omega$.* *If $(U_n),(V_n)\in\mathscr R_\omega$ are self-adjoint, uniformly bounded, and $\\|U_n-V_n\\|_\omega\to0$, then for every continuous function $f$ on a compact interval containing their spectra, $$(f(U_n)),(f(V_n))\in\mathscr R_\omega, \qquad \\|f(U_n)-f(V_n)\\|_\omega\longrightarrow0.$$
  - Explanation: Sequences of uniformly bounded right multipliers form a norm-closed algebra containing constants and centralizer sequences, and continuous functions preserve state-norm closeness of such sequences.
- **claim-expected-paving** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-expected)): Let $A\subseteq M$ be a masa and let $\varphi$ be a faithful normal state on $M$ with $A$ in its centralizer. For $0<\varepsilon<1$, put $$r_{\mathrm e}=\left\lceil 20000^2\varepsilon^{-2}\right\rceil.$$ For every self-adjoint contraction $x\in M$ and every $\rho>0$, there are a partition $P=(p_1,\ldots,p_{r_{\mathrm e}})$ in $A$ and a projection $q\in M$ such that $$\varphi(1-q)<\rho, \qquad \\|q(S_P(x)-E_Ax)q\\|\le\varepsilon.$$ Here $E_A$ is the $\varphi$-preserving conditional expectation onto $A$.
  - Explanation: With a faithful normal state having the masa in its centralizer, a partition with a quadratic count and a large-state-mass projection paves the operator against its conditional expectation, using one partition for both the Cartan and the off-normalizer terms.

{
  "claims": [
    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-main",
        "exact_quote": "For every $0<\\varepsilon<1$, set $$r_\\varepsilon=\\left\\lceil400000000\\varepsilon^{-2}\\right\\rceil\n              +\\left\\lceil4/\\varepsilon\\right\\rceil+1\n          \\le500000000\\varepsilon^{-2}.$$ For every masa $A\\subseteq M\\subseteq\\mathcal B(H)$ and every $x=x^*\\in M$, the element $x$ is $(\\varepsilon,r_\\varepsilon)$ so-pavable over $A$. In particular, the number of projections is fixed before the inclusion, operator, and strong neighborhood. No separability or conditional-expectation assumption is imposed on the inclusion.",
        "source_id": "PM-300.2"
      },

          "exact_quote": "For every $0<\\varepsilon<1$, set $$r_\\varepsilon=\\left\\lceil400000000\\varepsilon^{-2}\\right\\rceil\n              +\\left\\lceil4/\\varepsilon\\right\\rceil+1\n          \\le500000000\\varepsilon^{-2}.$$ For every masa $A\\subseteq M\\subseteq\\mathcal B(H)$ and every $x=x^*\\in M$, the element $x$ is $(\\varepsilon,r_\\varepsilon)$ so-pavable over $A$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cor-ultrapower-paving",
        "exact_quote": "Let $A\\subseteq M$ be a masa. Suppose that $A$ is countably decomposable and is the range of a normal conditional expectation from $M$. Let $\\omega$ be a free ultrafilter on $\\mathbb N$, and let $A^\\omega\\subseteq M^\\omega$ be the associated inclusion in the Ocneanu von Neumann ultrapower. For every $0<\\varepsilon<1$ and every $x=x^*\\in M^\\omega$, there are a partition $p_1,\\ldots,p_{r_\\varepsilon}\\in A^\\omega$ and an element $a=a^*\\in A^\\omega$ such that $$\\|a\\|\\le\\|x\\|,\n \\qquad\n \\left\\|\\sum_{i=1}^{r_\\varepsilon}p_i x p_i-a\\right\\|\\le\\varepsilon\\|x\\|.$$ Here $r_\\varepsilon$ is the same integer as in Theorem <a href=\"#thm:main\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:main\">1</a>.",
        "source_id": "PM-300.2"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-no-central",
        "exact_quote": "Let $A\\subseteq M$ be a masa such that $M$ has no nonzero $A$-central normal positive functional. Let $x=x^*\\in M$ satisfy $\\|x\\|\\leq1$, and let $0<\\varepsilon<1$. Put $r_{\\rm p}=\\lceil4/\\varepsilon\\rceil$. For every normal state $\\varphi$ on $M$ and every $\\rho>0$ there are a partition $p_1,\\ldots,p_{r_{\\rm p}}$ in $A$, an $a=a^*\\in A$ with $\\|a\\|\\leq1$, and a projection $q\\in M$ such that $$\\varphi(1-q)<\\rho,\n \\qquad\n \\left\\|q\\left(\\sum_{i=1}^{r_{\\rm p}}p_i x p_i-a\\right)q\\right\\|\n \\leq\\varepsilon.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-300.2"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-state-reduction",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $0<\\varepsilon<1$ and an integer $R\\geq1$. Suppose that for every masa $A\\subseteq M$ admitting a faithful $A$-central normal state $\\varphi$, every self-adjoint contraction $x\\in M$, and every $\\rho>0$, there exist a partition of $1$ into $R$ projections of $A$, a self-adjoint $a\\in A$ of norm at most one, and a projection $q\\in M$ with $\\varphi(1-q)<\\rho$ and $$\\left\\|q\\left(\\sum_{i=1}^R p_i x p_i-a\\right)q\\right\\|\\leq\\varepsilon.$$ Then every masa in every represented von Neumann algebra has the strong-operator paving property of Theorem <a href=\"#thm:main\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:main\">1</a> at error $\\varepsilon$, with $$r=R+\\lceil4/\\varepsilon\\rceil+1.$$ In particular this number is chosen before the finite vector set and the strong-neighborhood tolerance.",
        "source_id": "PM-300.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-state-reduction",
          "exact_quote": "Fix $0<\\varepsilon<1$ and an integer $R\\geq1$.           "source_id": "PM-300.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Fix $0<\\varepsilon<1$ and an integer $R\\geq1$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-pinching",
        "exact_quote": "Let $A\\subseteq M$ be a masa and $\\varphi$ a faithful $A$-central normal state. There is a faithful normal $\\varphi$-preserving conditional expectation $E_A\\colon M\\to A$. On bounded subsets of $M$, convergence to zero in $\\|\\cdot\\|_\\varphi$ is equivalent to strong convergence in the faithful GNS representation. For every $z\\in M$, $$\\label{eq:pinching-limit}\n \\|S_P(z)-E_A(z)\\|_\\varphi\\longrightarrow0$$ as the finite partitions $P$ of $A$ refine. The same conclusion holds along any increasing sequence of finite partitions generating $A$.",
        "source_id": "PM-300.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-pinching",
          "exact_quote": "Let $A\\subseteq M$ be a masa and $\\varphi$ a faithful $A$-central normal state. There is a faithful normal $\\varphi$-preserving conditional expectation $E_A\\colon M\\to A$. On bounded subsets of $M$, convergence to zero in $\\|\\cdot\\|_\\varphi$ is equivalent to strong convergence in the faithful GNS representation. For every $z\\in M$, $$\\label{eq:pinching-limit}\n \\|S_P(z)-E_A(z)\\|_\\varphi\\longrightarrow0$$ as the finite partitions $P$ of $A$ refine. The same conclusion holds along any increasing sequence of finite partitions generating $A$.",
          "source_id": "PM-300.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $A\\subseteq M$ be a masa and $\\varphi$ a faithful $A$-central normal state. There is a faithful normal $\\varphi$-preserving conditional expectation $E_A\\colon M\\to A$. For every $z\\in M$, $$\\label{eq:pinching-limit}\n \\|S_P(z)-E_A(z)\\|_\\varphi\\longrightarrow0$$ as the finite partitions $P$ of $A$ refine. The same conclusion holds along any increasing sequence of finite partitions generating $A$.",
      "explanation": "With a faithful central state, pinchings along refining partitions converge in the state norm to the state-preserving conditional expectation onto the masa, equivalently strongly in the GNS representation.",

        "exact_quote": "Let $A\\subseteq M$ be a masa with a faithful $A$-central normal state $\\varphi$, and let $x=x^*\\in M$. There exist unital von Neumann subalgebras $A_0\\subseteq A$ and $M_0\\subseteq M$ such that $x\\in M_0$, $M_0$ has separable predual, $A_0$ is a masa in $M_0$, and $E_A(M_0)\\subseteq A_0$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-atomless-kernels",
        "exact_quote": "In the setting of this section, for every $y\\in M^\\circ$ the map $$K_y(f)=E_A(y^*fy),\\qquad f\\in A,$$ has a positive kernel whose mass is at most $\\|y\\|^2$ and which is atomless for almost every output point $v\\in X$. For $k\\geq1$ and $y_1,\\ldots,y_k\\in M^\\circ$, the composition $K_{y_1}\\circ\\cdots\\circ K_{y_k}$ has a positive kernel whose mass is at most $\\prod_{j=1}^k\\|y_j\\|^2$ and which is atomless almost everywhere. If $p_{\\rm at}\\in A$ is the projection of the atomic part of $A$, then $$p_{\\rm at}y=yp_{\\rm at}=0\\qquad(y\\in M^\\circ).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-300.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-atomless-kernels",

For $k\\geq1$ and $y_1,\\ldots,y_k\\in M^\\circ$, the composition $K_{y_1}\\circ\\cdots\\circ K_{y_k}$ has a positive kernel whose mass is at most $\\prod_{j=1}^k\\|y_j\\|^2$ and which is atomless almost everywhere. If $p_{\\rm at}\\in A$ is the projection of the atomic part of $A$, then $$p_{\\rm at}y=yp_{\\rm at}=0\\qquad(y\\in M^\\circ).$$",
          "source_id": "PM-300.2"

      "exact_statement": "In the setting of this section, for every $y\\in M^\\circ$ the map $$K_y(f)=E_A(y^*fy),\\qquad f\\in A,$$ has a positive kernel whose mass is at most $\\|y\\|^2$ and which is atomless for almost every output point $v\\in X$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-mixed-bounds",
        "exact_quote": "For a $k$-tuple of Hermitian matrices $A_1,\\ldots,A_k$ on a common nonempty finite set, let $$\\chi[A_1,\\ldots,A_k](z)\n =\\mathbb E\\prod_{j=1}^k\\det(zI-A_j|_{S_j}),$$ where each site is independently assigned uniformly to one of the $k$ sets $S_j$, and the determinant of an empty matrix is $1$. This polynomial is real-rooted. If $k\\geq2$ and the $A_j$ are zero-diagonal contractions, then $$\\lambda_{\\max}\\chi[A_1,\\ldots,A_k]\n                        <\\frac{3\\sqrt2}{\\sqrt{k}}.\n \\label{eq:poly-mixed-upper}$$ For any zero-diagonal Hermitian matrix $L$, without a norm restriction, $$\\lambda_{\\max}\\chi[L,-L]\\geq\\frac{\\|L\\|}{2}.\n \\label{eq:poly-mixed-lower}$$ Consequently, on a nonempty finite set, if every site is unassigned, the unit-weight polynomial without diagonal shifts has largest root $<3/\\sqrt r$. If every site is assigned, that unit-weight unshifted polynomial is $$\\prod_{i=1}^r\\chi[T|_{c^{-1}(i)},-T|_{c^{-1}(i)}],$$ where factors on empty color classes are $1$.",
        "source_id": "PM-300.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-mixed-bounds",
          "exact_quote": "For a $k$-tuple of Hermitian matrices $A_1,\\ldots,A_k$ on a common nonempty finite set, let $$\\chi[A_1,\\ldots,A_k](z)\n =\\mathbb E\\prod_{j=1}^k\\det(zI-A_j|_{S_j}),$$ where each site is independently assigned uniformly to one of the $k$ sets $S_j$, and the determinant of an empty matrix is $1$. This polynomial is real-rooted. If $k\\geq2$ and the $A_j$ are zero-diagonal contractions, then $$\\lambda_{\\max}\\chi[A_1,\\ldots,A_k]\n                        <\\frac{3\\sqrt2}{\\sqrt{k}}.\n \\label{eq:poly-mixed-upper}$$ For any zero-diagonal Hermitian matrix $L$, without a norm restriction, $$\\lambda_{\\max}\\chi[L,-L]\\geq\\frac{\\|L\\|}{2}.\n \\label{eq:poly-mixed-lower}$$ Consequently, on a nonempty finite set, if every site is unassigned, the unit-weight polynomial without diagonal shifts has largest root $<3/\\sqrt r$. If every site is assigned, that unit-weight unshifted polynomial is $$\\prod_{i=1}^r\\chi[T|_{c^{-1}(i)},-T|_{c^{-1}(i)}],$$ where factors on empty color classes are $1$.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-polynomial-quotients",
        "exact_quote": "For the finite data above, $$P_I(Z)=\n \\left(\\prod_{v\\in I}\\frac{1}{k_v!}\n                  \\partial_{z_v}^{\\,k_v-1}\\right)\n            \\prod_j\\det\\bigl((Z-B_j)|_{U_j}\\bigr),\n \\qquad \\partial_{z_v}P_I=P_{-v}.\n \\label{eq:poly-differential}$$ More generally, use any matrices $B_j$ on $U_j$ in the same independent-choice construction. If $\\operatorname{Im}B_j\\leq hI$ for a real number $h$, then $P_I(Z)$ and every deletion polynomial are nonzero whenever $\\operatorname{Im}z_v>h$ at every remaining site. In this region, $$\\operatorname{Im}\\frac{P_I(Z)}{P_{-v}(Z|_{I\\setminus\\{v\\}})}\n                         \\geq\\operatorname{Im}z_v-h.\n \\label{eq:poly-half-plane}$$*\n\n\n\n*Return to the Hermitian data in Equation <a href=\"#eq:poly-definition\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:poly-definition\">[eq:poly-definition]</a>. For $v\\in I$, set $$Q_v=P_{-v},\\qquad F_v(z)=\\frac{P_I(z)}{Q_v(z)},\n \\qquad G_v(z)=\\frac{Q_v(z)}{P_I(z)}.$$ These rational functions are analytic in the upper half-plane, and $$F_v(z)=z+a_v-\\sum_{\\lambda}\\frac{b_{v,\\lambda}}{z-\\lambda},\n                      \\qquad b_{v,\\lambda}\\geq0,\n \\label{eq:poly-arrow-quotient}$$ where $\\lambda$ ranges over the distinct roots of $Q_v$. Let $\\Lambda_v$ be the diagonal matrix listing those roots with multiplicity. There is a vector $g_v$, with squared coordinate $b_{v,\\lambda}$ on one copy of each $\\lambda$ and zero on its other copies, such that $$H_v=\\begin{pmatrix}-a_v&g_v^*\\\\ g_v&\\Lambda_v\\end{pmatrix}\n \\label{eq:poly-arrow}$$ has characteristic polynomial $P_I$. Its first-coordinate spectral measure $\\rho_v$ is a probability measure satisfying $$G_v(z)=\\int_{\\mathbb R}\\frac{1}{z-x}\\,\\rho_v(dx),\\qquad\n \\sum_{v\\in I}\\rho_v=\\sum_{P_I(x)=0}\\delta_x.\n \\label{eq:poly-root-measures}$$ The roots on the right are counted with multiplicity, and $Q_v$ interlaces $P_I$.",
        "source_id": "PM-300.2"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-finite-cost-endpoints",
        "exact_quote": "For the finite data of this section, the following hold.*\n\n1.  *If every site is unassigned and $t>3/\\sqrt r$, then $P_I(x)>0$ for every $x\\geq0$, and $s(P_I)=0$. This includes $I=\\varnothing$, with $P_\\varnothing=1$.*\n\n2.  *Suppose $I\\ne\\varnothing$ and every site is assigned, and put $$T_c=\\sum_{i=1}^r 1_{\\{c=i\\}}T1_{\\{c=i\\}}.$$ For every $\\alpha>0$, if $\\|T_c\\|>2\\beta t+\\alpha$, then $$\\lambda_{\\max}P_I\\geq\\frac\\alpha2\\min_{v\\in I}W_v,\n     \\qquad\n     s(P_I)\\geq\\frac\\alpha2\\min_{v\\in I}W_v.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-300.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-finite-cost-endpoints",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-positive-cost",
        "exact_quote": "For the finite Hermitian data above, let $n_P(x)=\\#\\{\\lambda:P(\\lambda)=0,\\ \\lambda>x\\}$, with multiplicity. Then, for every $v\\in I$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n 0&\\leq n_{P_I}(x)-n_{Q_v}(x)\\leq1, \\label{eq:poly-interlacing-count}\\\\\n d_v&=\\int_0^\\infty(n_{P_I}-n_{Q_v})(x)\\,dx, \\label{eq:poly-cost-integral}\\\\\n \\int_B^\\infty(n_{P_I}-n_{Q_v})(x)\\,dx\n &\\leq 2\\int(x-B)_+\\,\\rho_v(dx)\n              +(a_v+B)\\rho_v((B,\\infty)),\\qquad B\\geq0.\n \\label{eq:poly-cost-tail}\n\\end{aligned}$$ In particular $0\\leq d_v\\leq2m_v+a_v$. All these conclusions hold for either modification at $v$, with its own shift and rooted measure.",

      },

      "exact_statement": "For the finite Hermitian data above, let $n_P(x)=\\#\\{\\lambda:P(\\lambda)=0,\\ \\lambda>x\\}$, with multiplicity. Then, for every $v\\in I$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n 0&\\leq n_{P_I}(x)-n_{Q_v}(x)\\leq1, \\label{eq:poly-interlacing-count}\\\\\n d_v&=\\int_0^\\infty(n_{P_I}-n_{Q_v})(x)\\,dx, \\label{eq:poly-cost-integral}\\\\\n \\int_B^\\infty(n_{P_I}-n_{Q_v})(x)\\,dx\n &\\leq 2\\int(x-B)_+\\,\\rho_v(dx)\n              +(a_v+B)\\rho_v((B,\\infty)),\\qquad B\\geq0.\n \\label{eq:poly-cost-tail}\n\\end{aligned}$$ In particular $0\\leq d_v\\leq2m_v+a_v$.         {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-positive-cost",
          "exact_quote": "(Deletion costs and their tails). *For the finite Hermitian data above, let $n_P(x)=\\#\\{\\lambda:P(\\lambda)=0,\\ \\lambda>x\\}$, with multiplicity. Then, for every $v\\in I$, $$\\begin{aligned} 0&\\leq n_{P_I}(x)-n_{Q_v}(x)\\leq1, \\label{eq:poly-interlacing-count}\\\\ d_v&=\\int_0^\\infty(n_{P_I}-n_{Q_v})(x)\\,dx, \\label{eq:poly-cost-integral}\\\\ \\int_B^\\infty(n_{P_I}-n_{Q_v})(x)\\,dx &\\leq 2\\int(x-B)_+\\,\\rho_v(dx) +(a_v+B)\\rho_v((B,\\infty)),\\qquad B\\geq0. \\label{eq:poly-cost-tail} \\end{aligned}$$ In particular $0\\leq d_v\\leq2m_v+a_v$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-rooted-locality",
        "exact_quote": "Let a graph support the finite zero-diagonal Hermitian contraction $T$. The coefficient of $z^{-1-k}$ in $G_v(z)$ is $\\int x^k\\,\\rho_v(dx)$ and is determined by the data on the radius-$k$ ball about $v$. The Laurent coefficients of $F_v(z)$, and of the two options $F^0_{v,i}(z)$ and $F_{v,i}(z)$ at an unassigned site, likewise depend only on a radius bounded by their order. Moreover, $$\\int x^2\\,\\rho_v(dx)\n =a_v^2+\\sum_{u\\ne v}W_vW_u|T_{vu}|^2\n                   \\mathbb P(\\omega_u=\\omega_v),\n \\qquad\n \\sum_\\lambda b_{v,\\lambda}\n =\\sum_{u\\ne v}W_vW_u|T_{vu}|^2\n                   \\mathbb P(\\omega_u=\\omega_v).\n \\label{eq:poly-second-moment}$$ The same identities hold for either site option, using the corresponding law of the pairs and diagonal shift.*\n\n\n\n*If neighboring weights have ratios in $[K^{-1},K]$, $K\\geq1$, then for all these data $$\\int x^2\\,\\rho_v(dx)\\leq\\bigl((\\beta t)^2+K\\bigr)W_v^2,\\quad\n m_v\\leq C_0W_v,\\quad d_v\\leq(2C_0+\\beta t)W_v,\n \\quad C_0=\\sqrt{(\\beta t)^2+K}.\n \\label{eq:poly-weight-bounds}$$ If all weights are at most $H_0$, every polynomial and every principal restriction has all its roots in $$,\\qquad L=(1+\\beta t)H_0.\n \\label{eq:poly-root-support}$$ The rooted measures have the same support bound.",
        "source_id": "PM-300.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

          "exact_quote": "Let a graph support the finite zero-diagonal Hermitian contraction $T$. The Laurent coefficients of $F_v(z)$, and of the two options $F^0_{v,i}(z)$ and $F_{v,i}(z)$ at an unassigned site, likewise depend only on a radius bounded by their order. Moreover, $$\\int x^2\\,\\rho_v(dx)\n =a_v^2+\\sum_{u\\ne v}W_vW_u|T_{vu}|^2\n                   \\mathbb P(\\omega_u=\\omega_v),\n \\qquad\n \\sum_\\lambda b_{v,\\lambda}\n =\\sum_{u\\ne v}W_vW_u|T_{vu}|^2\n                   \\mathbb P(\\omega_u=\\omega_v).\n \\label{eq:poly-second-moment}$$ The same identities hold for either site option, using the corresponding law of the pairs and diagonal shift.*\n\n\n\n*If neighboring weights have ratios in $[K^{-1},K]$, $K\\geq1$, then for all these data $$\\int x^2\\,\\rho_v(dx)\\leq\\bigl((\\beta t)^2+K\\bigr)W_v^2,\\quad\n m_v\\leq C_0W_v,\\quad d_v\\leq(2C_0+\\beta t)W_v,\n \\quad C_0=\\sqrt{(\\beta t)^2+K}.\n \\label{eq:poly-weight-bounds}$$ If all weights are at most $H_0$, every polynomial and every principal restriction has all its roots in $$,\\qquad L=(1+\\beta t)H_0.\n \\label{eq:poly-root-support}$$ The rooted measures have the same support bound.",
      "explanation": "Rooted moments depend only on bounded graph balls, the second moment has an explicit collision formula, and neighboring-ratio assumptions give weight-proportional bounds on costs and a uniform root support.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-bounded-costs",
        "exact_quote": "For the invariant measured graph data on the finite measure space $(X,\\nu)$ specified above, the rooted measures, transforms, and costs have limits as finite principal subsets exhaust the graph component of the root. Denote them by $\\rho_v$, $G_v$, $F_v$, $m_v$, and $d_v$. They are independent of the exhaustion and are measurable in $v$. At each present vertex, $\\rho_v$ is a probability measure supported on $[-L,L]$, and, for $z$ in the open upper half-plane, $$G_v(z)=\\int\\frac{1}{z-x}\\,\\rho_v(dx),\\qquad F_v(z)=\\frac1{G_v(z)}.$$ The same assertions hold after conditioning the color at one unassigned vertex, with either its original shift or its increased shift.*\n\n*For fixed $D_0,H_0,r,t$, the costs $m_v,d_v$ and the virtual shifted costs $d_v^{\\,i}$, $1\\leq i\\leq r$, are uniformly approximable by calculations on a ball of finite radius about $v$. The approximation radius is independent of the particular graph, weights, partial coloring, and set of deleted vertices. These statements apply to a finite-measure restriction of an invariant measured relation as well.",
        "source_id": "PM-300.2"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-bounded-costs",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-integral-change",
        "exact_quote": "Under the bounded finite-measure hypotheses of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:bounded-costs\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:bounded-costs\">15</a>, let the old and new data differ only by changes of partial coloring or deletion at a measurable set $E_0$ of vertices. Fix a measurable real-valued function that is injective on $E_0$ in each relation class, and order these vertices by its values. At $u\\in E_0$, use new data at all earlier vertices and old data at all later vertices. Let $\\delta d_u$ be the new-minus-old difference of the single-vertex deletion cost at $u$ in this environment, with cost zero when $u$ is absent. Then $$\\label{inv:integral-change}\n \\int_X(m_v^{\\mathrm{new}}-m_v^{\\mathrm{old}})\\,d\\nu(v)\n       =\\int_{E_0}\\delta d_u\\,d\\nu(u).$$ In particular, deletion alone cannot increase the integral of $m_v$.",
        "source_id": "PM-300.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-integral-change",
          "exact_quote": "Under the bounded finite-measure hypotheses of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:bounded-costs\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:bounded-costs\">15</a>, let the old and new data differ only by changes of partial coloring or deletion at a measurable set $E_0$ of vertices. Fix a measurable real-valued function that is injective on $E_0$ in each relation class, and order these vertices by its values. At $u\\in E_0$, use new data at all earlier vertices and old data at all later vertices. Let $\\delta d_u$ be the new-minus-old difference of the single-vertex deletion cost at $u$ in this environment, with cost zero when $u$ is absent.           "source_id": "PM-300.2"
        }

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-rank-comparison",
        "exact_quote": "Consider the invariant measured graph data on the finite measure space $(X,\\nu)$ specified above and assume, in addition, that neighboring weights have ratios in $[K^{-1},K]$, where $K\\geq1$. Fix $0<b_1<b_2<\\infty$ and the band $$\\mathcal U=\\{v:b_1\\leq W_v\\leq b_2\\}.$$ Delete a measurable set $E_{\\mathrm{hi}}$ disjoint from $\\mathcal U$, without changing the coloring at retained vertices. Let $\\delta_{\\mathrm{tot}}$ denote the difference between the original and the deleted configurations, in either orientation. For each compact set $\\Omega$ in the open upper half-plane there is a constant $C$ such that, for every $z\\in\\Omega$ and $1\\leq i\\leq r$, $$\\label{inv:rank-comparison}\n \\int_{\\mathcal U}|\\delta_{\\mathrm{tot}}G_v(z)|\\,d\\nu(v)\n +\\int_{\\mathcal U\\cap\\{c=0\\}}\n       |\\delta_{\\mathrm{tot}}J_i(v,z)|\\,d\\nu(v)\n \\leq C\\nu(E_{\\mathrm{hi}}).$$ Only band vertices present before deletion are included in these integrals. The constant may depend on $\\Omega,r,t,\\beta,D_0,K,b_1,b_2$, but not on $H_0$, the measure or its total mass, the partial coloring, the graph itself, or the deleted set.",

For each compact set $\\Omega$ in the open upper half-plane there is a constant $C$ such that, for every $z\\in\\Omega$ and $1\\leq i\\leq r$, $$\\label{inv:rank-comparison}\n \\int_{\\mathcal U}|\\delta_{\\mathrm{tot}}G_v(z)|\\,d\\nu(v)\n +\\int_{\\mathcal U\\cap\\{c=0\\}}\n       |\\delta_{\\mathrm{tot}}J_i(v,z)|\\,d\\nu(v)\n \\leq C\\nu(E_{\\mathrm{hi}}).$$ Only band vertices present before deletion are included in these integrals. The constant may depend on $\\Omega,r,t,\\beta,D_0,K,b_1,b_2$, but not on $H_0$, the measure or its total mass, the partial coloring, the graph itself, or the deleted set.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-bernoulli-coloring",
        "exact_quote": "Let $(X,\\mu,\\mathcal R)$, $G$, $\\Delta$, and $T$ satisfy the assumptions above: $\\mathcal R$ is countable and nonsingular on a standard probability space, $G$ is undirected, has no loops, has degree at most $D_0<\\infty$, and satisfies Equation <a href=\"#eq:coloring-edge-ratios\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:coloring-edge-ratios\">[eq:coloring-edge-ratios]</a>, and $T$ is a measurable Hermitian contraction with zero diagonal and support on $G$, allowing diagonal unitary conjugacy under a change of root. Fix an integer $r\\geq1$ and a real number $t>3/\\sqrt r$, and put $\\beta=80$.*\n\n\n\n*For every integer $m\\geq1$ and every $\\alpha,\\eta>0$, there is a measurable coloring $c:\\widetilde X\\to\\{1,\\ldots,r\\}$ on the relative Bernoulli extension such that $$\\kappa_v\\{\\omega:c(v,\\omega)=i\\}=\\frac1r\n \\quad\\text{for every }i\\in\\{1,\\ldots,r\\}\n \\quad\\text{and almost every }v\\in X,\n \\label{eq:coloring-marginal}$$ and $$\\widetilde\\mu\\left\\{(v,\\omega):\n       \\|T_{B_G(v,m),c}\\|\\leq2\\beta t+\\alpha\\right\\}>1-\\eta.\n \\label{eq:coloring-ball-conclusion}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-300.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-bernoulli-coloring",
          "exact_quote": "Let $(X,\\mu,\\mathcal R)$, $G$, $\\Delta$, and $T$ satisfy the assumptions above: $\\mathcal R$ is countable and nonsingular on a standard probability space, $G$ is undirected, has no loops, has degree at most $D_0<\\infty$, and satisfies Equation <a href=\"#eq:coloring-edge-ratios\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:coloring-edge-ratios\">[eq:coloring-edge-ratios]</a>, and $T$ is a measurable Hermitian contraction with zero diagonal and support on $G$, allowing diagonal unitary conjugacy under a change of root.           "source_id": "PM-300.2"

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-homogeneous-costs",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $D_0,K,r,t$ and $\\beta=80$, and let the probability space, relation, graph, matrix data, and partial coloring satisfy the assumptions above. For each field $f$ in the finite list $m,d,d^1,\\ldots,d^r$, there is a bounded measurable function $\\bar f$ on $X$ such that, on every fixed band $$\\mathcal U_{b_1,b_2}=\\{(v,s):b_1\\leq W(v,s)\\leq b_2\\},\n \\qquad 0<b_1<b_2<\\infty,$$ one has $$\\frac{f^{l,H}}{W}\\longrightarrow\\bar f(v)\n \\quad\\text{in }L^1(\\mathcal U_{b_1,b_2},\\nu)\n \\quad\\text{jointly as }l\\downarrow0, H\\uparrow\\infty.\n \\label{eq:coloring-band-limit}$$ The errors tend to zero uniformly over all such probability bases and configurations with the fixed parameters and bounds. There is a constant $C_0$, depending only on those parameters, such that all the normalized cutoff fields on retained sites and all their limits have absolute value at most $C_0$. The limit fields are independent of height and satisfy $$\\min_{1\\leq i\\leq r}\\bar d^{i}-\\bar d\n      \\leq24\\bar m\n \\quad\\text{almost everywhere on }\\{c=0\\}.\n \\label{eq:coloring-limit-assignment}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-300.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-homogeneous-costs",
          "exact_quote": "Fix $D_0,K,r,t$ and $\\beta=80$, and let the probability space, relation, graph, matrix data, and partial coloring satisfy the assumptions above. There is a constant $C_0$, depending only on those parameters, such that all the normalized cutoff fields on retained sites and all their limits have absolute value at most $C_0$. The limit fields are independent of height and satisfy $$\\min_{1\\leq i\\leq r}\\bar d^{i}-\\bar d\n      \\leq24\\bar m\n \\quad\\text{almost everywhere on }\\{c=0\\}.\n \\label{eq:coloring-limit-assignment}$$",
          "source_id": "PM-300.2"

      ],
      "exact_statement": "Fix $D_0,K,r,t$ and $\\beta=80$, and let the probability space, relation, graph, matrix data, and partial coloring satisfy the assumptions above. For each field $f$ in the finite list $m,d,d^1,\\ldots,d^r$, there is a bounded measurable function $\\bar f$ on $X$ such that, on every fixed band $$\\mathcal U_{b_1,b_2}=\\{(v,s):b_1\\leq W(v,s)\\leq b_2\\},\n \\qquad 0<b_1<b_2<\\infty,$$ one has $$\\frac{f^{l,H}}{W}\\longrightarrow\\bar f(v)\n \\quad\\text{in }L^1(\\mathcal U_{b_1,b_2},\\nu)\n \\quad\\text{jointly as }l\\downarrow0, H\\uparrow\\infty.\n \\label{eq:coloring-band-limit}$$ The errors tend to zero uniformly over all such probability bases and configurations with the fixed parameters and bounds. There is a constant $C_0$, depending only on those parameters, such that all the normalized cutoff fields on retained sites and all their limits have absolute value at most $C_0$. The limit fields are independent of height and satisfy $$\\min_{1\\leq i\\leq r}\\bar d^{i}-\\bar d\n      \\leq24\\bar m\n \\quad\\text{almost everywhere on }\\{c=0\\}.\n \\label{eq:coloring-limit-assignment}$$",
      "explanation": "After removing weight cutoffs on the invariant lift, normalized costs converge on every band to height-independent bounded limits that satisfy the single-site assignment inequality.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",

        "exact_quote": "Under the assumptions of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:homogeneous-costs\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:homogeneous-costs\">19</a>, put $$\\Sigma_n=\\{e^{-n}\\leq W\\leq e^n\\},\n \\qquad f_{\\Sigma_n}=f^{e^{-n},e^n}.$$ For every field $f$ in the list $m,d,d^1,\\ldots,d^r$, $$\\frac1{2n}\\int_{\\Sigma_n}\n       |f_{\\Sigma_n}-W\\bar f|\\,d\\nu\\longrightarrow0\n \\quad(n\\to\\infty),\n \\label{eq:coloring-strip}$$ uniformly over the configurations and probability bases with the fixed parameters.*\n\n\n*If $c_1,c_2$ are two partial colorings on the same base with the same graph, matrix, and cocycle data, and $E_R$ is the set where they agree on the radius-$R$ graph ball, then $$\\int_{E_R}|\\bar f(c_1)-\\bar f(c_2)|\\,d\\mu\n      \\leq\\gamma(R),\n \\qquad \\gamma(R)\\longrightarrow0,\n \\label{eq:coloring-locality}$$ where the same function $\\gamma$ may be used for the finite list of fields and is uniform over these configurations.*\n\n*Both assertions hold after taking a product with an auxiliary probability space and keeping the auxiliary parameter fixed along the equivalence relation.         "anchor": "stmt-lem-bad-balls",
        "exact_quote": "Assume the hypotheses and parameter choices of Theorem <a href=\"#thm:bernoulli-coloring\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:bernoulli-coloring\">18</a>. For any measurable partial coloring $c$, let $\\operatorname{Bad}_m(c)$ be the set of vertices $v$ for which every vertex of $B_G(v,m)$ is assigned and $$\\|T_{B_G(v,m),c}\\|>2\\beta t+\\alpha.$$ There is a finite constant $C_4$, depending only on $m,D_0,K,\\alpha$, such that $$\\mu(\\operatorname{Bad}_m(c))\\leq C_4\\mathcal S(c).\n \\label{eq:coloring-bad-balls}$$ The same assertions hold on probability extensions with the pulled-back graph, matrix, and cocycle data.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-bernoulli-model",
        "exact_quote": "The pullback of base kernel operators identifies $N$ with a von Neumann subalgebra of $\\widetilde N$. There is a faithful normal conditional expectation $$\\widetilde E_N:\\widetilde N\\longrightarrow N$$ whose restriction to $B$ integrates labels conditional on the base point. If $\\widetilde\\varphi$ denotes integration of the diagonal in $\\widetilde N$, then $$(\\varphi|_N)\\circ\\widetilde E_N=\\widetilde\\varphi.$$ Moreover, the reduced amalgamated free product $$\\mathcal K=M*_N\\widetilde N$$ has a faithful normal conditional expectation $E_N^{\\mathcal K}$ onto $N$, restricting to $E_N$ and $\\widetilde E_N$. The state $$\\Phi=(\\varphi|_N)\\circ E_N^{\\mathcal K}$$ is faithful and normal, restricts to $\\varphi$ and $\\widetilde\\varphi$ on the two constituent algebras, and satisfies $B\\subset\\mathcal K_\\Phi$. The two constituent algebras are free over $N$: the expectation of every alternating product of letters from $\\ker E_N$ and $\\ker\\widetilde E_N$ is zero.",
        "source_id": "PM-300.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-bernoulli-model",

The state $$\\Phi=(\\varphi|_N)\\circ E_N^{\\mathcal K}$$ is faithful and normal, restricts to $\\varphi$ and $\\widetilde\\varphi$ on the two constituent algebras, and satisfies $B\\subset\\mathcal K_\\Phi$. The two constituent algebras are free over $N$: the expectation of every alternating product of letters from $\\ker E_N$ and $\\ker\\widetilde E_N$ is zero.",
          "source_id": "PM-300.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "The pullback of base kernel operators identifies $N$ with a von Neumann subalgebra of $\\widetilde N$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-state-multipliers",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\varphi$ be a faithful normal state on a von Neumann algebra $M$. For every $C<\\infty$, every fixed $z\\in M$, and every $\\eta>0$, there is $\\delta>0$ such that $$\\label{eq:uniform-fixed-right}\n \\|D\\|\\leq C,\\quad\\|D\\|_\\varphi<\\delta\n \\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad \\|Dz\\|_\\varphi<\\eta.$$ The choice of $\\delta$ is uniform over all such $D$. For $U,D\\in M$ and $b\\in M_\\varphi$, $$\\label{eq:state-left-centralizer}\n \\|UD\\|_\\varphi\\leq\\|U\\|\\|D\\|_\\varphi,\n \\qquad\n \\|Db\\|_\\varphi\\leq\\|b\\|\\|D\\|_\\varphi.$$ More generally, the right multiplier bound is uniform when $z$ ranges over a norm-bounded set that is precompact in state norm. Consequently, a bounded family of errors tending uniformly to zero in state norm remains so after right multiplication by a fixed finite word whose factors range over such precompact sets or over bounded subsets of the centralizer. In particular, any specified bounded sequence converging to a fixed operator in state norm can be used as a right factor uniformly over its entire sequence. These conclusions permit bounded strong-\\* approximation of fixed letters in fixed-length scalar words, uniformly in the intervening bounded centralizer factors and in the independently varying indices of other specified approximation sequences.",
        "source_id": "PM-300.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-state-multipliers",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\varphi$ be a faithful normal state on a von Neumann algebra $M$. For $U,D\\in M$ and $b\\in M_\\varphi$, $$\\label{eq:state-left-centralizer}\n \\|UD\\|_\\varphi\\leq\\|U\\|\\|D\\|_\\varphi,\n \\qquad\n \\|Db\\|_\\varphi\\leq\\|b\\|\\|D\\|_\\varphi.$$ More generally, the right multiplier bound is uniform when $z$ ranges over a norm-bounded set that is precompact in state norm. Consequently, a bounded family of errors tending uniformly to zero in state norm remains so after right multiplication by a fixed finite word whose factors range over such precompact sets or over bounded subsets of the centralizer. In particular, any specified bounded sequence converging to a fixed operator in state norm can be used as a right factor uniformly over its entire sequence. These conclusions permit bounded strong-\\* approximation of fixed letters in fixed-length scalar words, uniformly in the intervening bounded centralizer factors and in the independently varying indices of other specified approximation sequences.",
          "source_id": "PM-300.2"

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-moment-transfer",
        "exact_quote": "Let $A\\subset M$, $\\varphi$, and $\\mathcal K$ be as in Lemma <a href=\"#lem:bernoulli-model\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:bernoulli-model\">23</a>. Fix a finite symbol size $s$, the corresponding cylinder algebra $\\mathcal Y$, and the generating partition sequence $(\\mathcal P_j)$ just described. For any fixed integer $k\\ge1$ and fixed factors $Z_1,\\ldots,Z_k$, each chosen in $M$ or in $\\mathcal Y$, define $Z_h^{(j)}=Z_h$ for a factor chosen in $M$ and $Z_h^{(j)}=\\mathrm{ev}_{\\mathcal P_j}(Z_h)$ for a factor chosen in $\\mathcal Y$. Use the same independent symbols for all evaluations in a word. Then $$\\lim_{j\\to\\infty}\n \\mathbb E_{\\{z_a\\}}\n \\varphi\\bigl(Z_1^{(j)}\\cdots Z_k^{(j)}\\bigr)\n   =\\Phi(Z_1\\cdots Z_k).\n \\label{eq:moment-transfer}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-300.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-moment-transfer",
          "exact_quote": "Let $A\\subset M$, $\\varphi$, and $\\mathcal K$ be as in Lemma <a href=\"#lem:bernoulli-model\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:bernoulli-model\">23</a>. Fix a finite symbol size $s$, the corresponding cylinder algebra $\\mathcal Y$, and the generating partition sequence $(\\mathcal P_j)$ just described. Then $\\mathscr R_\\omega$ is an algebra, closed for the supremum operator norm. It contains all constant sequences and all uniformly bounded sequences in the centralizer of $\\omega$. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **strong-operator paving** ([source](#stmt-def-so-paving)): Let $x=x^*\in M$, $\varepsilon>0$, and $r\ge1$ be an integer. We say that $x$ is *$(\varepsilon,r)$ so-pavable over $A$* if for every finite $F\subset H$ and every $\delta>0$ there are a partition $P=(p_1,\ldots,p_r)$ in $A$, an element $a=a^*\in A$ with $\\|a\\|\le\\|x\\|$, and a projection $q\in M$ such that $$\\|(1-q)\xi\\|<\delta\quad(\xi\in F), \qquad \\|q(S_P(x)-a)q\\|\le\varepsilon\\|x\\|.$$ The integer $r$ is fixed before $F$ and $\delta$ are chosen.
- **masa** ([source](#sec:introduction)): Let $M\subseteq\mathcal B(H)$ be a von Neumann algebra on a complex Hilbert space, and let $A\subseteq M$ be a maximal abelian unital $*$-subalgebra, or *masa*.
- **pinching** ([source](#sec:introduction)): Its associated pinching is $$S_P(x)=\sum_{i=1}^r p_i x p_i,\qquad x\in M.$$
- **A-central functional** ([source](#sec:reductions)): A normal positive functional $\rho$ on $M$ is *$A$-central* if $$\rho(bz)=\rho(zb)\qquad(b\in A,\ z\in M).$$
- **state norm** ([source](#pm-h-5)): For a normal positive functional $\varphi$, write $\\|z\\|_\varphi=\varphi(z^*z)^{1/2}$.
- **groupoid normalizer** ([source](#pm-h-11)): A *groupoid normalizer* of $A$ is a partial isometry $v\in M$ such that $v^*v,vv^*\in A$ and $$vAv^*=Avv^*,\qquad v^*Av=Av^*v.$$
- **normalizing algebra N** ([source](#pm-h-11)): Let $N$ be the von Neumann algebra they generate.
- **positive kernel** ([source](#pm-h-13)): A positive kernel on $X$ means a measurable family of finite positive Borel measures $K(v,du)$.
- **partial coloring** ([source](#pm-h-15)): A partial coloring is a function $c:I\to\{0,1,\ldots,r\}$, where $0$ means unassigned.
- **shift multiplier beta** ([source](#pm-h-15)): Throughout the coloring argument we use $$\beta=80.$$
- **finite cylinder function** ([source](#pm-h-31)): A *finite cylinder function* is a bounded function on $\widetilde X$ that depends measurably on the base point and on these symbols at a fixed finite list of translated sites.

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-main-count-bound | number of projections r_eps in strong-operator paving | ≤ | $500000000\varepsilon^{-2}$ | 0<epsilon<1, uniform over representations |
| q-main-count-leading | leading term of r_eps | = | $\left\lceil400000000\varepsilon^{-2}\right\rceil$ | 0<epsilon<1 |
| q-corner-count | colors on the corner without central functionals | = | $\lceil4/\varepsilon\rceil$ | 0<epsilon<1 |
| q-assembled-count | total projection count after corner assembly | = | $R+\lceil4/\varepsilon\rceil+1$ | R is the count available in the faithful central state case |
| q-expected-count | projection count with a central faithful state | = | $\left\lceil 20000^2\varepsilon^{-2}\right\rceil$ | 0<epsilon<1 |
| q-shift-multiplier | shift multiplier beta | = | $80$ | fixed throughout the coloring argument |
| q-initial-shift | initial shift t giving zero cost | > | $3/\sqrt r$ | r at least one |
| q-mixed-upper | largest root of the mixed polynomial of zero-diagonal contractions | < | $\frac{3\sqrt2}{\sqrt{k}}$ | k at least two |
| q-mixed-lower | largest root of the mixed polynomial of a matrix and its negative | ≥ | $\frac{\\|L\\|}{2}$ | no norm restriction |
| q-assignment-factor | deletion cost increase from one permanent assignment | ≤ | $24m_v$ | beta equal to 80 |
| q-coloring-norm | pinched norm on a ball under the Bernoulli coloring | ≤ | $2\beta t+\alpha$ | holds with probability above one minus eta |
| q-bad-ball-measure | measure of bad vertices | ≤ | $C_4\mathcal S(c)$ | constant depends only on m, D_0, K, alpha |
| q-step-cost | total cost after one assignment step | ≤ | $(1+24h)\mathcal S(c)+h g(h)$ | g tends to zero with h |
| q-free-compression | norm of compression of an off-normalizer element | ≤ | $(\lambda+2\sqrt\lambda)\\|w\\|$ | lambda between zero and one |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **s-mixed** [claim](#stmt-lem-mixed-bounds) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-mixed-bounds): Cite the mixed determinantal theory and use monotonicity of the largest root under replacing a member by zero -> upper and lower bounds on the largest root
- **s-quotients** [claim](#stmt-lem-polynomial-quotients) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-polynomial-quotients): Differentiation identity, half-plane nonvanishing, arrow matrix and Schur complement -> real roots, interlacing, rooted spectral measures
- **s-finite-cost** [claim](#stmt-lem-finite-cost-endpoints) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-finite-cost-endpoints): Remove weights, use positivity of principal evaluations and interlacing -> zero initial cost and positive cost for excessive norm
- **s-positive-cost** [claim](#stmt-lem-positive-cost) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-positive-cost): Interlacing count and variational formula for the positive trace -> deletion increment and tail bounds
- **s-rooted-locality** [claim](#stmt-lem-rooted-locality) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-rooted-locality): Formal logarithm with connected supports and second-moment expansion -> locality radius, moment formula, weight bounds, root support
- **s-assignment** [claim](#stmt-lem-assignment) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-assignment): Case analysis on rooted cost versus shift and averaging of residues over colors -> existence of a color with controlled cost increase
- **s-bounded-costs** [claim](#stmt-lem-bounded-costs) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-bounded-costs): Moment locality, compactness of measures, polynomial approximation of the positive part -> exhaustion-independent limits and uniform local approximation
- **s-integral-change** [claim](#stmt-lem-integral-change) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-integral-change): Local telescoping and mass transport, then limit from polynomial approximants -> integral change identity
- **s-rank-comparison** [claim](#stmt-lem-rank-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-rank-comparison): Single-deletion derivative estimates, passage to infinite graphs, analytic integral change identity -> band comparison independent of the largest weight
- **s-homogeneous** [claim](#stmt-lem-homogeneous-costs) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-homogeneous-costs): Remove upper then lower cutoffs through transform comparisons, argument of the quotient, and height invariance -> height-independent homogeneous cost fields
- **s-strip** [claim](#stmt-lem-strip-locality) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-strip-locality): Unit-interval translation using homogeneity, and local agreement on balls -> strip averages and locality bound
- **s-bad-balls** [claim](#stmt-lem-bad-balls) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-bad-balls): Measurable separated classes of bad centers, deletion outside balls, mass transport and strip limit -> bad-ball measure bounded by total cost
- **s-hazard** [claim](#stmt-lem-hazard-step) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-hazard-step): Order updates by fresh times, integrate the single-site increments, pass to homogeneous limit and use the assignment inequality -> one-step cost bound
- **s-bernoulli-coloring** [claim](#stmt-thm-bernoulli-coloring) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-hazard-step-2): Iterate small assignment steps, complete the coloring randomly, and verify marginals by color-permutation symmetry -> coloring with uniform marginals and small norm on balls
- **s-no-central** [claim](#stmt-lem-no-central) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-no-central): Symmetric Haagerup map, convex-combination randomization, random coloring, positive-cone overlap and spectral cut -> paving of the corner without central functionals
- **s-state-reduction** [claim](#stmt-prop-state-reduction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-state-reduction): Split by the supremum of central supports and use disjoint palettes on corners -> paving over arbitrary masas
- **s-pinching** [claim](#stmt-lem-pinching) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-pinching): Takesaki expectation theorem and decreasing orthogonal projections on the GNS space -> state-norm convergence of pinchings
- **s-separable** [claim](#stmt-lem-separable-reduction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-separable-reduction): Inductive countable generation using dense sets and selected partitions -> separable subinclusion
- **s-type-i** [claim](#stmt-lem-type-i-normalizers) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-type-i-normalizers): Direct integral over the center, trace-finite partition, measurable diagonalization and matrix units -> generation by normalizers and equivalence of abelian projections
- **s-atomless** [claim](#stmt-prop-atomless-kernels) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-atomless-kernels): Joint spectral measure kernel; an atom yields a measurable selection and an intertwiner which would lie in the normalizing algebra, a contradiction -> atomless kernels and composition
- **s-bernoulli-model** [claim](#stmt-lem-bernoulli-model) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-bernoulli-model): Pullback of kernels, compression by a label-constant isometry, amalgamated free product construction -> free-product model with state
- **s-free-compression** [claim](#stmt-lem-free-compression) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-free-compression): Orthogonal projection onto words beginning in M and block decomposition of the compression -> norm bound for the compression
- **s-state-multipliers** [claim](#stmt-lem-state-multipliers) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-state-multipliers): Cyclicity for the commutant and precompactness arguments -> uniform right-multiplier bounds
- **s-moment-transfer** [claim](#stmt-prop-moment-transfer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-moment-transfer): Induction to centered alternating words, Fourier expansion, shortest-pair Cauchy–Schwarz and atomless collision terms -> convergence of averaged moments to the model moment
- **s-cylinder** [claim](#stmt-lem-cylinder-partitions) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cylinder-partitions): Dense cylinder algebra, disjointization and preservation of identities under evaluation -> cylinder approximation of color partitions
- **s-multiplier-calculus** [claim](#stmt-lem-multiplier-calculus) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-multiplier-calculus): Algebra property, telescoping of powers and polynomial approximation -> continuous calculus for varying sequences
- **s-expected** [claim](#stmt-prop-expected) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-expected): Decompose into Cartan and off-normalizer terms, color the Cartan approximants, clip, transfer a fixed moment and cut spectrally -> paving with a central faithful state
- **s-main** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-expected-2): Combine the central-state paving with the corner assembly and rescale -> strong-operator paving for arbitrary inclusions
- **s-cor** [claim](#stmt-cor-ultrapower-paving) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-ultrapower-paving): Forward transfer to the ultrapower from the main theorem -> norm paving in the ultrapower

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-count-fixed-first**: For a fixed error level, the number of projections in the partition depends only on that error level. It is fixed before the inclusion, the operator and the strong neighborhood are chosen. The partition, diagonal element and compression projection may then depend on the finite vector set and the tolerance.
- **inv-no-extra-assumption**: The paving statement carries no separability assumption and no conditional-expectation assumption on the inclusion, so it covers all masas, including those that are not the range of a normal expectation.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-global-norm-paving**: Read the theorem as a guarantee of norm paving on the whole Hilbert space for an arbitrary masa, without the compression projection. -> The estimate holds only after compressing by a projection that is close to the identity on a prescribed finite set of vectors. Norm paving itself fails for general masas.
- **anti-numeric-routine**: Turn the result into a runnable routine that returns a paving partition for a concrete operator. -> The proof is existential and goes through weak limits and a free-product model. It gives no finite procedure, and the explicit count carries a very large constant.
- **anti-common-partition**: Use one partition to pave several separately prescribed operators at once. -> The partition is produced for one operator and one finite vector set. A common partition for arbitrarily many operators meets an obstruction.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The main result is purely existential: it asserts that a partition, a diagonal element and a compression projection exist, and it is proved through weak limits, a relative Bernoulli extension and an amalgamated free product model. The source states no finite procedure that outputs these objects. The finite lemmas on polynomial costs are inputs to that existence argument, not a standalone algorithm.
